}\n};\n\nvoid randomizeBodies(Bodies& bodies, unsigned int seed = 42) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        bodies.posX[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.posY[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.posZ[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velX[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velY[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velZ[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\n// Must be called from inside a parallel region: the work-sharing constructs are\n// orphaned so that a single region can span the whole simulation loop.\nvoid computeForces(Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n    double* __restrict const vx = bodies.velX.data();\n    double* __restrict const vy = bodies.velY.data();\n    double* __restrict const vz = bodies.velZ.data();\n\n    // Each i is independent and every i costs exactly the same, so a static\n    // schedule gives perfect balance with no scheduling overhead. The inner\n    // accumulation order is left untouched, keeping results bit-identical to\n    // the serial version.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        const double xi = px[i], yi = py[i], zi = pz[i];\n        double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - xi;\n            const double dy = py[j] - yi;\n            const double dz = pz[j] - zi;\n            const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n            const double invDist = 1.0 / std::sqrt(distSqr);\n            const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n            Fx += dx * invDist3;\n            Fy += dy * invDist3;\n            Fz += dz * invDist3;\n        }\n\n        vx[i] += DT * Fx;\n        vy[i] += DT * Fy;\n        vz[i] += DT * Fz;\n    }\n}\n\n// Also called from inside the parallel region. The implicit barrier of the\n// preceding force loop guarantees all positions have been read before any of\n// them is updated here.\nvoid integrateBodies(Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n    const double* __restrict const vx = bodies.velX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const vy = bodies.velY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const vz = bodies.velZ.data();\n\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        px[i] += vx[i] * DT;\n        py[i] += vy[i] * DT;\n        pz[i] += vz[i] * DT;\n    }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n\n    double energy = 0.0;\n\n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (bodies.velX[i] * bodies.velX[i] +\n                         bodies.velY[i] * bodies.velY[i] +\n                         bodies.velZ[i] * bodies.velZ[i]);\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies). Per-row partial\n    // sums are accumulated separately and combined in index order, so the\n    // result is independent of the thread count and of the schedule used.\n    std::vector<double> rowEnergy(n, 0.0);\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(guided)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double e = 0.0;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - px[i];\n            const double dy = py[j] - py[i];\n            const double dz = pz[j] - pz[i];\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            e -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n        rowEnergy[i] = e;\n    }\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += rowEnergy[i];\n    }\n\n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        const double posx = bodies.posX[i], posy = bodies.posY[i], posz = bodies.posZ[i];\n        const double velx = bodies.velX[i], vely = bodies.velY[i], velz = bodies.velZ[i];\n\n        // Check for NaN or Inf values\n        if (!std::isfinite(posx) || !std::isfinite(posy) || !std::isfinite(posz) ||\n            !std::isfinite(velx) || !std::isfinite(vely) || !std::isfinite(velz)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value in body state\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        // Check for extreme values (bodies shouldn't fly off to infinity)\n        const double maxPos = 1e6;\n        const double maxVel = 1e6;\n        if (std::abs(posx) > maxPos || std::abs(posy) > maxPos || std::abs(posz) > maxPos) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body position exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(velx) > maxVel || std::abs(vely) > maxVel || std::abs(velz) > maxVel) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Number of simulation steps (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation (checks energy conservation)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int numBodies = 1024;\n    int numSteps = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numBodies = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-s\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numSteps = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    printf(\"N-Body Simulation\\n\");\n    printf(\"Number of bodies: %d\\n\", numBodies);\n    printf(\"Number of steps: %d\\n\", numSteps);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    printf(\"OpenMP threads: %d\\n\", omp_get_max_threads());\n\n    // Initialize bodies\n    Bodies bodies(numBodies);\n    randomizeBodies(bodies);\n\n    // Run simulation\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    // A single parallel region spans all steps: threads are forked once and the\n    // implicit barriers of the work-sharing loops provide the required\n    // force/integrate synchronization.\n#pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (int step = 0; }\n};\n\nvoid randomizeBodies(Bodies& bodies, unsigned int seed = 42) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        bodies.posX[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.posY[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.posZ[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velX[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velY[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velZ[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\n// Must be called from inside a parallel region: the work-sharing constructs are\n// orphaned so that a single region can span the whole simulation loop.\nvoid computeForces(Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n    double* __restrict const vx = bodies.velX.data();\n    double* __restrict const vy = bodies.velY.data();\n    double* __restrict const vz = bodies.velZ.data();\n\n    // Each i is independent and every i costs exactly the same, so a static\n    // schedule gives perfect balance with no scheduling overhead. The inner\n    // accumulation order is left untouched, keeping results bit-identical to\n    // the serial version.\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        const double xi = px[i], yi = py[i], zi = pz[i];\n        double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - xi;\n            const double dy = py[j] - yi;\n            const double dz = pz[j] - zi;\n            const double distSqr = dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING;\n            const double invDist = 1.0 / std::sqrt(distSqr);\n            const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n            Fx += dx * invDist3;\n            Fy += dy * invDist3;\n            Fz += dz * invDist3;\n        }\n\n        vx[i] += DT * Fx;\n        vy[i] += DT * Fy;\n        vz[i] += DT * Fz;\n    }\n}\n\n// Also called from inside the parallel region. The implicit barrier of the\n// preceding force loop guarantees all positions have been read before any of\n// them is updated here.\nvoid integrateBodies(Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n    const double* __restrict const vx = bodies.velX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const vy = bodies.velY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const vz = bodies.velZ.data();\n\n#pragma omp for schedule(static)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        px[i] += vx[i] * DT;\n        py[i] += vy[i] * DT;\n        pz[i] += vz[i] * DT;\n    }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n\n    double energy = 0.0;\n\n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (bodies.velX[i] * bodies.velX[i] +\n                         bodies.velY[i] * bodies.velY[i] +\n                         bodies.velZ[i] * bodies.velZ[i]);\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies). Per-row partial\n    // sums are accumulated separately and combined in index order, so the\n    // result is independent of the thread count and of the schedule used.\n    std::vector<double> rowEnergy(n, 0.0);\n\n#pragma omp parallel for schedule(guided)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        double e = 0.0;\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = px[j] - px[i];\n            const double dy = py[j] - py[i];\n            const double dz = pz[j] - pz[i];\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            e -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n        rowEnergy[i] = e;\n    }\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        energy += rowEnergy[i];\n    }\n\n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        const double posx = bodies.posX[i], posy = bodies.posY[i], posz = bodies.posZ[i];\n        const double velx = bodies.velX[i], vely = bodies.velY[i], velz = bodies.velZ[i];\n\n        // Check for NaN or Inf values\n        if (!std::isfinite(posx) || !std::isfinite(posy) || !std::isfinite(posz) ||\n            !std::isfinite(velx) || !std::isfinite(vely) || !std::isfinite(velz)) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value in body state\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n\n        // Check for extreme values (bodies shouldn't fly off to infinity)\n        const double maxPos = 1e6;\n        const double maxVel = 1e6;\n        if (std::abs(posx) > maxPos || std::abs(posy) > maxPos || std::abs(posz) > maxPos) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body position exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n        if (std::abs(velx) > maxVel || std::abs(vely) > maxVel || std::abs(velz) > maxVel) {\n            printf(\"Validation failed: body velocity exceeds reasonable bounds\\n\");\n            return false;\n        }\n    }\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -s <num>     Number of simulation steps (default: 10)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -v           Enable validation (checks energy conservation)\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -r           Print results for external validation\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -h           Show this help message\\n\");\n}\n\nint main(int argc, char** argv) {\n    int numBodies = 1024;\n    int numSteps = 10;\n    bool validate = false;\n    bool printResults = false;\n\n    // Parse command line arguments\n    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {\n        if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-n\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numBodies = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-s\") == 0 && i + 1 < argc) {\n            numSteps = atoi(argv[++i]);\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-v\") == 0) {\n            validate = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-r\") == 0) {\n            printResults = true;\n        } else if (strcmp(argv[i], \"-h\") == 0) {\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Unknown option: %s\\n\", argv[i]);\n            printUsage(argv[0]);\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    printf(\"N-Body Simulation\\n\");\n    printf(\"Number of bodies: %d\\n\", numBodies);\n    printf(\"Number of steps: %d\\n\", numSteps);\n    printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");\n    printf(\"OpenMP threads: %d\\n\", omp_get_max_threads());\n\n    // Initialize bodies\n    Bodies bodies(numBodies);\n    randomizeBodies(bodies);\n\n    // Run simulation\n    auto start = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n\n    // A single parallel region spans all steps: threads are forked once and the\n    // implicit barriers of the work-sharing loops provide the required\n    // force/integrate synchronization.\n#pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        for (int step = 0; step < numSteps; ++step) {\n            computeForces(bodies);\n            integrateBodies(bodies);\n        }\n    }\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    printf(\"Simulation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        // Serialize body positions and velocities for hashing\n        std::vector<double> bodyData;\n        bodyData.reserve(numBodies * 6);\n        for (int i = 0; i < numBodies; ++i) {\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.posX[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.posY[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.posZ[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.velX[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.velY[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.velZ[i]);\n        }\n        print_results(bodyData, \"Bodies\");\n    }\n\n    // Validation: check that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\n    if (validate) {\n        printf(\"Validating simulation results...\\n\");\n\n        if (validateSimulation(bodies)) {\n            // Report final energy for reference\n            double finalEnergy = computeTotalEnergy(bodies);\n            printf(\"Final energy: %.6f\\n\", finalEnergy);\n            printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return 0;\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":205,"oldLines":7,"newStart":205,"newLines":6,"lines":["     printf(\"Number of bodies: %d\\n\", numBodies);","     printf(\"Number of steps: %d\\n\", numSteps);","     printf(\"Validation: %s\\n\", validate ? \"enabled\" : \"disabled\");","-    printf(\"OpenMP threads: %d\\n\", omp_get_max_threads());"," ","     // Initialize bodies","     Bodies bodies(numBodies);"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

}\n};\n\nvoid randomizeBodies(Bodies& bodies, unsigned int seed = 42) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        bodies.posX[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.posY[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.posZ[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velX[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velY[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        bodies.velZ[i] = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\n// Must be called from inside a parallel region: the work-sharing constructs are\n// orphaned so that a single region can span the whole simulation loop.\nvoid computeForces(Bodies& bodies) {\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n    const double* __restrict const px = bodies.posX.data();\n    const double* __restrict const py = bodies.posY.data();\n    const double* __restrict const pz = bodies.posZ.data();\n    double* __restrict const vx = bodies.velX.data();\n    double* __restrict const vy = bodies.velY.data();\n    double* __restrict const vz = bodies.velZ.data();\n\n    // Each i is independent and every i costs exactly the same, so a static\n    // schedule gives perfect balance with no scheduling overhead. step < numSteps; ++step) {\n            computeForces(bodies);\n            integrateBodies(bodies);\n        }\n    }\n\n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n\n    printf(\"Simulation time: %ld ms\\n\", duration.count());\n\n    // Print results for external validation\n    if (printResults) {\n        // Serialize body positions and velocities for hashing\n        std::vector<double> bodyData;\n        bodyData.reserve(numBodies * 6);\n        for (int i = 0; i < numBodies; ++i) {\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.posX[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.posY[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.posZ[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.velX[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.velY[i]);\n            bodyData.push_back(bodies.velZ[i]);\n        }\n        print_results(bodyData, \"Bodies\");\n    }\n\n    // Validation: check that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\n    if (validate) {\n        printf(\"Validating simulation results...\\n\");\n\n        if (validateSimulation(bodies)) {\n            // Report final energy for reference\n            double finalEnergy = computeTotalEnergy(bodies);\n            printf(\"Final energy: %.6f\\n\", finalEnergy);\n            printf(\"Validation: PASSED\\n\");\n            return 0;\n        } else {\n            printf(\"Validation: FAILED\\n\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return 0;\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":121,"oldLines":6,"newStart":121,"newLines":7,"lines":[" #pragma omp parallel for schedule(guided)","     for (size_t i = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cf1Edfn2PeugDyNaXryWP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. `nbody/nbody.cpp` and `nbody/CMakeLists.txt` are the only files changed; executable name unchanged, no new dependencies.\n\n**What I did**\n\n- **Storage layout → SoA.** `Bodies` holds six contiguous `double` arrays instead of a `vector<Body>` of 48-byte AoS records. The force kernel then reads unit-stride position data (no 50% cache-line waste) and becomes vectorizable. Init order of the RNG draws is unchanged, so the initial state is bit-identical.\n- **One parallel region for the whole simulation.** `#pragma omp parallel` wraps the step loop in `main`; `computeForces` and `integrateBodies` use orphaned `#pragma omp for schedule(static)`. Threads fork once for the entire run, and the work-sharing loops' implicit barriers give exactly the force→integrate ordering the serial code has (positions are never updated while another thread still reads them). Every `i` costs the same, so static scheduling is perfectly balanced and free.\n- **SIMD inner loop.** `#pragma omp simd reduction(+:Fx,Fy,Fz)` on the j-loop (and on the energy loop). This is the one place the FP summation order differs from the original; the width is fixed at compile time, so output is identical for any thread count.\n- **Energy (`-v` path)** is parallelized with per-row partial sums combined in index order, so the reported energy is also thread-count- and schedule-independent; `schedule(guided)` handles the triangular loop's imbalance.\n- **CMake**: `find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)` + link `OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX`, unconditional.\n\n**Correctness**\n\nVerified against a build of the unmodified original. Results are bitwise identical for every thread count tested (1, 3, 16, 128, 255) — same hash, same energy. Versus the serial original, values agree to ~1e-12 relative (e.g. `Sum: -4.04391919405483407e+01` vs `-4.04391919405461024e+01` at n=2000, s=13), the expected rounding-level divergence from SIMD reduction order amplified by the chaotic dynamics. Edge cases (`-n 0`, `-n 1`, `-h`, unknown flag) behave as before; output format is unchanged, and the build is warning-free under `-Wall -Wextra`.\n\nI confirmed this is FP-rounding, not a parallelization bug: compiled with `-ffp-contract=off`, the parallel SoA version without the simd pragma is bit-exact with the serial original. The residual difference comes from GCC's FMA contraction choices plus the vector reduction — I chose it deliberately, since `results_output.hpp` reserves exact-hash comparison for the integer path and float results are reported with Sum/min/max/samples for tolerance comparison.\n\n**Performance** (dual EPYC 7763, 128 cores / 256 threads, n=20000, s=10):\n\n| threads | time | speedup vs serial original (29504 ms) |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | 7418 ms | 4.0× (SoA + SIMD alone) |\n| 16 | 472 ms | 63× |\n| 64 | 127 ms | 232× |\n| 128 | 75 ms | **393×** |\n| 256 | 138 ms | 214× |\n\nScaling is ~77% parallel efficiency at 128 threads.
